    - Stop early: put the rest of the line into the `children` of a condition (or an `ELSE`), so it runs only when it should.
    - Give data to a function: set variables right before `CALL_FUNCTION` (with `%player_uuid%` or `%uuid%` in the names when several targets run it).

## 14. Making a game: rules that keep it working

Read this before you write a game. Every rule here comes from games that broke in real tests.

Plan first, in a few lines, before the JSON:
- which event starts what; which variables are global and which per player (`%player_uuid%`); which must be saved;
- what happens when: the world starts, a second player joins, a playing player types `/play`, a player dies or leaves, someone wins;
- what you cannot test yourself (coordinates, how a model looks). Say it in your answer.

### 14.1 Start, restart and cleanup

1. `PLAYER_JOIN` runs for EVERY player and again on every `/play`. One-time setup of a round (reset counters, start loops, spawn things) needs a guard: a variable `phase` without `save`. Start the round only when `phase` < 1, set `phase` to 1 there, and set it back to 0 when the round ends. A code restart deletes `phase`, so a restart always gives a fresh round (Example E).
2. Do not put the setup only into `PLOT_WORLD_STARTS`. It does not run when the first player enters an empty world (section 5.9), and empty worlds are unloaded and loaded again often. `PLAYER_JOIN` always runs.
3. `/play` by a playing player does not reset variables. If a new round should start then, reset the round in `PLAYER_JOIN` behind the `phase` guard, as in Example E. Every restart (also an import) drops waiting `GAME_WAIT`s: never take something from a player and give the result after a long wait; take and give in the same run.
4. Set every scoreboard line and bossbar title when a round starts (section 5.9). The line text of a scoreboard is the line id: keep the same text (`§fKills`) and change only the score. A new text adds a second line.
5. Entities stay forever (section 5.9). Give every entity you spawn a name, and remove the old ones when a round starts and when it ends. Put it into its own function, so the select inside does not change the selection of the caller:
   `{"kind":"function","name":"remove_zombies","nodes":[{"action":"SELECT_MOBS_BY_CONDITION","condition":{"action":"IF_ENTITY_NAME_EQUALS","args":{"name":"Wave zombie"}}},{"action":"ENTITY_REMOVE"}]}`
   For visualizers use `SELECT_ENTITIES_BY_CONDITION` (visualizers are not mobs).

### 14.2 Players and items

6. The inventory is empty after a player enters play mode (section 5.9). Give the game items in `PLAYER_JOIN`. Items bought or won during the game are lost the same way on the next entry (and on `/play`): keep what a player owns in saved variables (for example `kit_%player_uuid%`) and give it again in `PLAYER_JOIN`.
7. On death the items drop by default. Put `PLAYER_SET_DEATH_DROPS` with `enable` 0 into `PLAYER_JOIN` to keep them, or give the items again in `PLAYER_DEATH` (the player is already alive at the spawn when it runs).
8. To recognise your custom item, check the same `item` and `components` you gave with `IF_PLAYER_HOLDING_ITEM`, with `durability` 1 (tools lose durability when used) and `state` 1 (main hand). In `PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK` put `GAME_CANCEL_EVENT` first when the click must not do its normal thing. A pressure plate fires `PLAYER_BLOCK_INTERACT` (action `PHYSICAL`) again and again while the player stands on it: keep the last plate location in a per-player variable and act only when it changes.
9. Cooldown: keep the "ready at" time in a per-player variable. Set it with `VARIABLE_SUM` (`number` = `{"val":"CURRENT_UNIX_TIME"}`, `numbers` = the cooldown in milliseconds) and allow the action only when `CURRENT_UNIX_TIME` >= that variable (`IF_VARIABLE_EASY_COMPARE_NUMBER` with `state` 1). A missing variable reads as 0, so the first use is always allowed. Example E does it.

### 14.3 Mobs

10. A spawn location must be free: two air blocks above a solid block. A mob spawned inside blocks suffocates at once, and its death still fires `ENTITY_DEATH`. The ground of a plot is not flat everywhere, so never guess coordinates. The spawn of the world is free (players stand there). For an arena somewhere else, ask the player for the coordinates.
11. Whole coordinates are block corners: `{"loc":[10,64,10]}` puts a mob on the corner of four blocks. Add `.5` for the center of a block: `{"loc":[10.5,64,10.5]}`.
12. Zombies, skeletons and some other mobs burn in daylight. Make it night (`GAME_TIME` with `time` 18000 and `state` 1, and `GAME_SET_GAME_RULE_VALUE` rule 6 = `false` so it stays night) or give them a helmet (`helmet` of `GAME_SPAWN_MOB`).
13. Use the `far_away` marker 1 on `GAME_SPAWN_MOB`, otherwise mobs far from players may despawn.
14. Waves: before a new wave, count the living mobs of the game (select them by name, then `VARIABLE_INCREMENT`, section 8) and spawn only when the count is 0. Without the check a loop spawns new mobs on every run until they kill the player. Mobs can also walk away or get stuck far from the arena, and then the count never reaches 0: give a wave a time limit (count the loop runs in a variable and send the next wave after N runs anyway), and remove the old named mobs when a new wave starts.
15. Counting kills:
    - `PLAYER_KILL_MOB` fires only when the last damage was a direct hit by the player.
    - Damage done by code (`PLAYER_DAMAGE`, `GAME_CREATE_EXPLOSION`) has no attacker, so those kills fire only `ENTITY_DEATH`.
    - `ENTITY_DEATH` fires for EVERY death of a mob: suffocation, falling, fire, the void too. Count only mobs with your name (`IF_ENTITY_NAME_EQUALS`) and only while the round runs (`phase`).
    - `ENTITY_REMOVE` is not a death: removed mobs fire no death event.
16. `GAME_CREATE_EXPLOSION` also hurts the player who caused it. Near players keep `power` at 1, and use the `break` marker 1 so the map is not destroyed.

### 14.4 Selections in games

17. In a loop or a world event, start with `SELECT_RANDOM_PLAYER` (to run once) or `SELECT_ALL_PLAYERS`. With nobody playing, the selection is empty and nothing runs.
18. After a select that may find nothing (mobs in a radius, players by a condition), all following nodes of that list do nothing, also the children of brackets and of their `ELSE`. Select again (`SELECT_DEFAULT_PLAYER` in player events, `SELECT_RANDOM_PLAYER` in loops) before the nodes that must still run, or move the mob part into a function.
19. The `targets` line of every action says whom it works on. Actions with `targets: players` (messages, titles, items, sounds, particles) skip mobs in the selection; actions with `players and other living entities` or `any selected entity` (`PLAYER_DAMAGE`, `PLAYER_HEAL`, `PLAYER_LAUNCH_*`) work on mobs too. To send a message to the event player while mobs are selected, add `"target":"DEFAULT"` to that node.

### 14.5 Visualizers (display entities)

20. A block display shows a whole block with its normal texture. A model made of scaled planks looks like planks, not like the thing it should be. For a real-looking object use the real item in an item display (`GAME_SPAWN_DISPLAY_ITEM` with `{"item":"minecraft:chest"}` shows a real chest). Build a model from parts only when it needs moving parts, and pick for each part a block whose texture fits it.
21. Geometry of a block display: the block fills the cube from the location to location + 1. `ENTITY_SET_DISPLAY_SCALE` grows it from that corner, rotations turn it around that corner, `ENTITY_SET_DISPLAY_TRANSLATION` moves it together with the corner. To hinge a lid on its back edge, move the lid's corner onto the hinge with `translation` and rotate around the X axis (`ENTITY_SET_DISPLAY_AXIS_ROTATION`, axis `{"vec":[1,0,0]}`).
22. To animate a change, run `ENTITY_SET_DISPLAY_INTERPOLATION` (`duration` in ticks, `delay` 0) on the same selection right before the change, every time. Without it the change jumps.
23. Name every part, remove the old parts before you spawn new ones (rule 5), and spawn them in `PLAYER_JOIN` behind the `phase` guard. Every display counts toward the entity limit of the plot.

### 14.6 Testing

24. After the import, read the chat: `error executing ...` lines name the action that failed while running.
25. Test the whole path of a player: join, use the item, win, die, `/play` again, a second player joining. If you cannot test something, say so in your answer.

### 14.7 Vectors and vector weapons

Facts:
- Axes: +X = east, -X = west, +Y = up, +Z = south, -Z = north. A VECTOR is always 3 numbers: `{"vec":[x,y,z]}`.
- The look direction of a player is `{"val":"CURRENT_EYE_VIEW"}` (length 1, includes looking up or down). The eye position is `{"val":"CURRENT_EYE_LOCATION"}`. There is no value called `CURRENT_DIRECTION`.
- Speed is in blocks per tick (20 ticks = 1 second). A walking player moves about 0.2, an arrow about 3. Every `PLAYER_LAUNCH_*` action REPLACES the velocity, and the velocity is scaled down so that no part is above 4.
- There is no action that takes a VECTOR as a velocity. Split the vector with `VARIABLE_GET_COORDINATES_OF_VECTOR` and give the parts to `PLAYER_LAUNCH_TOWARD`. To move a location along a vector, split it the same way and add the parts with `VARIABLE_SET_LOCATION_VALUES` (`xState`, `yState`, `zState` = 1).
- Vector actions run once for EACH selected target (section 5.1), and every run writes the same output variable. When you compute a vector per mob, put `%uuid%` (the target the node runs for) into the variable names, as in recipe 3.
- A zero vector has no direction: `VARIABLE_SET_LENGTH_OF_VECTOR` then does not change the variable.
- `VARIABLE_ROTATE_VECTOR_AROUND_AXIS` with axis Y and a positive angle turns east toward north. That is the opposite of a growing yaw: to turn a direction like yaw + 30, use angle -30.

These three recipes were tested on the server. Put each list into the `children` of an `IF_PLAYER_HOLDING_ITEM` in `PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK`.

1. Dash forward, flat (ignores looking up or down):
```json
{"action":"VARIABLE_HADAMARD_PRODUCT_OF_VECTORS","args":{"variable":{"var":"dash"},"vector":{"val":"CURRENT_EYE_VIEW"},"vector2":{"vec":[1,0,1]}}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_SET_LENGTH_OF_VECTOR","args":{"variable":{"var":"dash"},"vector":{"var":"dash"},"length":1.5}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_GET_COORDINATES_OF_VECTOR","args":{"vector":{"var":"dash"},"x":{"var":"dx"},"z":{"var":"dz"}}},
{"action":"PLAYER_LAUNCH_TOWARD","args":{"x":{"var":"dx"},"y":0.3,"z":{"var":"dz"}}}
```
   Result: about 2 to 7 blocks forward; ground friction stops it sooner, in the air it flies farther. For a dash that follows the look up and down, use `PLAYER_LAUNCH_FORWARD` instead.

2. Beam that damages the first mob in the look direction:
```json
{"action":"VARIABLE_SET","args":{"variable":{"var":"eye"},"values":{"val":"CURRENT_EYE_LOCATION"}}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_MULTIPLY_VECTOR_BY_NUMBER","args":{"variable":{"var":"ray"},"vector":{"val":"CURRENT_EYE_VIEW"},"number":30}},
{"action":"PLAYER_SHOW_RAY_OF_PARTICLES","args":{"particle":{"particle":"minecraft:crit"},"location":{"var":"eye"},"vector":{"var":"ray"},"number":60},"switches":{"state":0}},
{"action":"SELECT_MOBS_BY_CONDITION","condition":{"action":"IF_ENTITY_IS_MOB"}},
{"action":"SELECT_FILTER_BY_RAY_TRACE","args":{"start":{"var":"eye"},"length":30,"width":0.3,"limit":1}},
{"action":"PLAYER_DAMAGE","args":{"damage":8}}
```
   `eye` keeps the yaw and pitch of the look, and the ray direction of `SELECT_FILTER_BY_RAY_TRACE` comes from them. Save the eye location BEFORE the select: after `SELECT_MOBS_BY_CONDITION` a game value without `sel` would be read for a mob. The ray particles come first for the same reason (they are shown to the selected player). The ray vector must be long (look direction × 30): the vector length is the ray length.

3. Shockwave: push every mob within 6 blocks away from the player:
```json
{"action":"VARIABLE_SET","args":{"variable":{"var":"center"},"values":{"val":"CURRENT_LOCATION"}}},
{"action":"SELECT_MOBS_BY_CONDITION","condition":{"action":"IF_ENTITY_IS_MOB"}},
{"action":"SELECT_FILTER_BY_DISTANCE","args":{"center":{"var":"center"},"radius":6}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_CREATE_VECTOR_BETWEEN_LOCATIONS","args":{"variable":{"var":"kb_%uuid%"},"location":{"var":"center"},"location2":{"val":"CURRENT_LOCATION"}}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_HADAMARD_PRODUCT_OF_VECTORS","args":{"variable":{"var":"kb_%uuid%"},"vector":{"var":"kb_%uuid%"},"vector2":{"vec":[1,0,1]}}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_SET_LENGTH_OF_VECTOR","args":{"variable":{"var":"kb_%uuid%"},"vector":{"var":"kb_%uuid%"},"length":1.5}},
{"action":"VARIABLE_GET_COORDINATES_OF_VECTOR","args":{"vector":{"var":"kb_%uuid%"},"x":{"var":"kx_%uuid%"},"z":{"var":"kz_%uuid%"}}},
{"action":"PLAYER_LAUNCH_TOWARD","args":{"x":{"var":"kx_%uuid%"},"y":0.5,"z":{"var":"kz_%uuid%"}}}
```
   `CURRENT_LOCATION` in the 4th node is read for each mob, `center` is the player. Each mob gets its own `kb_%uuid%`, so the pushes do not overwrite each other. A mob at exactly the player's position has no direction and is not pushed.

### 14.8 Clean and fast code

The server runs the code of many worlds at once and gives every world a small budget (section 5.8). Code that wastes it gets cut and finally stops. Write it like this:

Structure:
- One line, one job. An event line reacts to the event and calls functions; the real work lives in functions with names that say what they do (`start_round`, `give_kit`, `next_wave`).
- Never copy the same nodes into several lines. Put them into a function and call it. A fix then happens in one place, and the 23-lines-per-floor budget lasts longer.
- Keep one game's variables together with one short prefix (`zh_kills`, `zh_phase`) and per-player data with `%player_uuid%` in the name. Use the same name and the same `save` everywhere.
- Save (`save:true`) only what must survive a restart: records, coins, unlocked things. Round data stays unsaved, so a restart cleans it.
- There is no action that deletes a variable, so do not make new variable names without need (for example one per round). Keep per-player data that comes and goes in ONE dict keyed by `%player_uuid%` and remove the key with `DICT_REMOVE_BY_KEY` when it is not needed any more; clear lists and dicts that only grow. The number of variables and their memory are limited.

Speed:
- Do the cheapest check first. In fast events (`PLAYER_MOVE`, `PLAYER_TURNS_AROUND`, `PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK`, damage events) and in fast loops, start with a guard condition (is the round running? does the player hold the item?) and put the work into its `children`, so most runs end after one node.
- Prefer an event to a loop that checks all the time: a pressure plate, a click or a death fires exactly when needed.
- Give loops the largest `ticks` that still feels right: 20 (once per second) for timers and scoreboards, 5–10 only for things that must look smooth. Use one loop for many things instead of many loops, and stop loops when the round ends.
- Select only what you need: mobs by name, players by distance or condition. Every action runs once per selected target, so `SELECT_ALL_ENTITIES` in a loop multiplies the work by every entity of the world.
- Compute a value once and keep it in a variable instead of computing it again in every node or every run (an arena center, a list of spawn points, an item).
- Update what players see only when it changes: set the scoreboard score when a kill happens, not in every loop run. Messages, titles and action bars count toward the message limit; once per second is plenty for a status line.
- Do not spawn without a limit. Count your mobs before spawning more (14.3 rule 14), and remove entities you no longer need. Entities and particles are limited per world.
- Do big block work in parts: one fill is at most 5000 blocks, and the world has a budget of 5000 blocks per second. Split a big build into boxes with a `GAME_WAIT` of 1 second between them.

### 14.9 When the code hits a limit

What you see: the owner gets a chat message that names the limit (actions, messages, new variables, teleports, explosions, timers, particles, CPU load...) with the numbers. The run of the line that went over the limit stops at that node; the rest of that run does not happen. If it happens too often, the world is switched to build mode. Particles and blocks over their budget are not shown or placed.

What to do, in this order:
1. Find the line. The message names the limit, not the line. Look for the line that runs most often or touches the most targets: a fast loop, `PLAYER_MOVE`, a loop with `SELECT_ALL_*`, a function that calls itself.
2. Do less per run: narrow the selection, put a guard condition first, remove repeated work (a value computed again and again, world work done once per player in a loop when once is enough: `SELECT_RANDOM_PLAYER` first).
3. Run less often: a bigger `ticks`, an event instead of a loop, a cooldown on things players can spam.
4. Spread big work over time: instead of doing 500 things in one run, do 50 per loop run (keep an index in a variable), or split with `GAME_WAIT`.
5. Clean up: remove old entities, clear lists and dictionaries you no longer need.
6. Size limits on import (`TOO_MANY_HANDLERS`, `LINE_TOO_WIDE`, `TOO_MANY_NODES`): move repeated parts into functions, merge lines that do the same thing with different numbers into one function that reads the numbers from variables, and ask the player whether the plot has more code floors.
Always make the code lighter. Do not help anyone to get around limits, protections or the rules of the server.

### 14.10 How the game feels to the player

Players judge a game by what they see and hear, not by the code:
- Chat is the worst place for game information: it scrolls away and mixes with other messages. Show the state in the action bar (`PLAYER_ACTION_BAR`) or on a scoreboard, big moments (start, new wave, win) with a title, and information about a place right at that place (a text visualizer or a hologram). Use chat for rules at the start and for results.
- Every action of the player gets an answer: a sound and a short action bar text. Use different sounds for good and bad results, for example `entity.experience_orb.pickup` for success and `entity.villager.no` for "not allowed / not ready" (`PLAYER_SOUND`). Frequent sounds quieter, rare ones louder.
- Update a status line at most once per second, and only when it changed.
- Write the color code before the format code: `§6§lWIN`, not `§l§6WIN` (a color code resets bold).
- Particles and visualizers on events (a hit, a click, a win) with small counts, not in a loop every tick. Spawn visualizers when something changes, not again and again.
- Use the hotbar for real tools of the game (a weapon, a menu item), not for many buttons.
- Remove debug messages (`PLAYER_MESSAGE` with variable values) before you give the final code: every player sees them.

# Catalog

Every id below is exact. Use only these ids, argument keys and marker keys.

## Events

An event line starts with `{"kind":"event","event":"<ID>","nodes":[...]}`. This catalog lists every event id in the server order.

How to read an entry:

- `default target`: the starting selection of the line. The server puts exactly one target into the selection: the default target of the event. `%default%`, node `"target":"DEFAULT"` and game value `"sel":"DEFAULT"` mean the same target.
  - "the world default" means: one player of the world (the first one in the world's player list; it is NOT the player who caused the event and it is not random). This list also has players who are coding or building in the code area; such a player is removed from the selection, and then the selection is empty. If the world has no players, the default target is the last entity spawned in the world. If there is none, the selection is empty.
  - "empty" means the line starts with no targets. Actions that work per target do nothing. Use a `SELECT_*` action first, or `"target":"ALL"` on a node.
- `cancellable: yes` means `GAME_CANCEL_EVENT` stops the game action (the block is not placed, the damage is not dealt, and so on). Cancel BEFORE any `GAME_WAIT`: after a wait the game event is already over.
- `event values`: game values (`{"val":"EVENT_..."}`) that give data of this event. They return 0, empty text, an air item or nothing in events that do not give them.
- Event entities. Some events also remember other entities. Read them like this:
  - the event entity: node `"target":"DEFAULT_ENTITY"` (only IF_ENTITY conditions), game value `"sel":"DEFAULT_ENTITY"`, placeholder `%entity%`. Action `SELECT_DEFAULT_ENTITY` selects it.
  - killer / damager / shooter / projectile: node `"target":"KILLER"` (`DAMAGER`, `SHOOTER`; `PROJECTILE` only for IF_ENTITY), game value `"sel":"KILLER"` (and so on), placeholders `%killer%`, `%damager%`, `%shooter%`, `%projectile%`.
  - victim: there are two kinds. The "combat victim" is set only in damage, kill, death and knockback events; node `"target":"VICTIM"` uses ONLY the combat victim. The plain victim is set in more events (clicked entity, leashed entity, vehicle, and so on); game value `"sel":"VICTIM"` and `%victim%` use the plain victim. In damage and kill events both are the same entity. Each entry says which ones are set.
  - In events whose category starts with `PLAYER_`, IF_ENTITY conditions without `target` test the event entity, if the event has one.
- Common rules for all events:
  - Code runs only while the world is in play mode.
  - Player events fire only for a player who is in play mode in the plot world (not for players in the code area).
  - World and entity events fire only for blocks and entities in the plot world.
  - Several handlers of the same event all run, in line order.

### PLAYER_JOIN
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who joined | cancellable: no
Fires when a player enters play mode in this world: the player joins the world while it is in play mode, the world is switched to play mode (it fires for every player who is not coding), the player comes back from the code area with `/play`, or the player types `/play` again while playing.
notes:
- When the world is switched to play mode, the code restarts first, then PLAYER_JOIN runs for each player, then PLOT_WORLD_STARTS runs once.
- `/play` while already playing gives PLAYER_REJOIN, then PLAYER_QUIT, then PLAYER_JOIN. A coder who types `/play` gets only PLAYER_JOIN (the code is restarted first).
- This is the best place to start loops and create bossbars and scoreboards (see the guide, section 5.7).
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_JOIN","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§aWelcome, %player%!"}}]}

### PLAYER_QUIT
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who leaves | cancellable: no
Fires when a player in play mode stops playing in this world: the player leaves the world or the server, goes to the code area with `/dev`, the world is switched to build mode, or the player types `/play` again.
notes:
- The player is still in the world when the line runs, so you can still read the player's values and send messages.
- Do not use `GAME_WAIT` here: after the wait the player is usually gone and the rest of the line does nothing.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_QUIT","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§e%player% left the game"}}]}

### PLAYER_REJOIN
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who typed /play | cancellable: no
Fires when a player who is already playing types `/play` (restart for this player). Right after it come PLAYER_QUIT and PLAYER_JOIN for the same player.
notes:
- Does not fire for a player who types `/play` from the code area.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_REJOIN","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§7You restarted the game."}}]}

### PLAYER_BLOCK_INTERACT
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires on every interaction of the player's main hand: left or right click on a block or in the air, and physical actions (stepping on a pressure plate, trampling farmland).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item in the hand (air if empty); EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the clicked block as an item (nothing for air clicks); EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the clicked block; EVENT_INTERACT_ACTION (TEXT): `LEFT_CLICK_BLOCK`, `RIGHT_CLICK_BLOCK`, `LEFT_CLICK_AIR`, `RIGHT_CLICK_AIR` or `PHYSICAL`; EVENT_BLOCK_FACE (TEXT): clicked side of the block (`UP`, `DOWN`, `NORTH`, `SOUTH`, `EAST`, `WEST`, `SELF`)
notes:
- Off-hand interactions are ignored, so a click fires once. The hand is read with the condition IF_PLAYER_EQUIPMENT_SLOT_EQUALS; here it is always the main hand (no hand for PHYSICAL).
- Very fast clicks are not all passed to code.
- The same click also fires PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK or PLAYER_LEFT_CLICK (this event runs first).
- Fires often (every click, every step on a plate). Keep the line short.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BLOCK_INTERACT","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7%value(EVENT_INTERACT_ACTION)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player right-clicks a block or the air with the main hand (`RIGHT_CLICK_BLOCK` or `RIGHT_CLICK_AIR`).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item in the hand (air if empty); EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the clicked block as an item (nothing for air clicks); EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the clicked block
notes:
- The Minecraft client sends a right click into the air only when the player holds an item.
- Holding the right button down can fire it several times.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK","nodes":[{"action":"IF_PLAYER_HOLDING_ITEM","args":{"items":{"item":"minecraft:blaze_rod"}},"children":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§6Magic!"}}]}]}

### PLAYER_LEFT_CLICK
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player left-clicks a block or the air with the main hand (`LEFT_CLICK_BLOCK` or `LEFT_CLICK_AIR`).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item in the hand (air if empty)
notes:
- Hitting an entity is not a left click here (it is a damage event).
- EVENT_BLOCK is not given by this event; use PLAYER_BLOCK_INTERACT if you need the block.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_LEFT_CLICK","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§cLeft click"}}]}

### PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK_ENTITY
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who clicked | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player right-clicks an entity with the main hand.
notes:
- Event entity (`DEFAULT_ENTITY`, `%entity%`) = the clicked entity. Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the clicked entity. Node `"target":"VICTIM"` is empty here.
- IF_ENTITY conditions without `target` test the clicked entity.
- No event values.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_RIGHT_CLICK_ENTITY","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§7You clicked §e%entity%"}}]}

### PLAYER_LEASH_ENTITY
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player puts a lead on an entity.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item the player is using right now (often air)
notes:
- Event entity = the player. Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the leashed entity.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_LEASH_ENTITY","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§7You leashed §e%victim%"}}]}

### PLAYER_TAME_ENTITY
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who tamed | cancellable: yes
Fires when a player tames an animal (wolf, cat, horse and so on).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item the player is using right now (often air)
notes:
- Event entity = the player. Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the tamed animal.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_TAME_ENTITY","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§aYou tamed §e%victim%"}}]}

### PLAYER_BLOCK_PLACE
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player places a block, after the server allowed it (it does not fire if the placement was already blocked).
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the placed block as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the placed block; EVENT_BLOCK_FACE (TEXT): always `SELF`
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BLOCK_PLACE","nodes":[{"action":"IF_GAME_BLOCK_EQUALS","args":{"blocks":{"item":"minecraft:tnt"},"location":{"val":"EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION"}},"children":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"},{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§cTNT is not allowed here."}}]}]}

### PLAYER_BLOCK_BREAK
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player breaks a block, after the server allowed it.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the broken block as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the broken block; EVENT_BLOCK_FACE (TEXT): side of the block that faces the player's feet block, empty text when they do not touch (usually empty)
notes:
- The game value CURRENT_TARGET_BLOCK_LOCATION returns the broken block's location in this event.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BLOCK_BREAK","nodes":[{"action":"VARIABLE_INCREMENT","args":{"variable":{"var":"broken_%player_uuid%","save":true}}},{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Blocks broken: §e%var(broken_%player_uuid%)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_FISHING
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the fishing player | cancellable: yes
Fires on every stage of fishing: casting the rod, a bite, catching a fish, hooking an entity, the hook landing on the ground, reeling in without a catch.
event values: EVENT_FISHING_STATE (TEXT): `FISHING`, `CAUGHT_FISH`, `CAUGHT_ENTITY`, `IN_GROUND`, `FAILED_ATTEMPT`, `REEL_IN`, `BITE`; EVENT_FISHING_CAUGHT (ITEM): the caught item when the state is `CAUGHT_FISH`, otherwise air; EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item the player is using right now
notes:
- Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the caught entity (the item entity for a fish), if any.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_FISHING","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§bFishing: %value(EVENT_FISHING_STATE)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_BLOCK_DAMAGE
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player starts to break a block (first hit on it), if the server allowed it.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the block as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the block
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BLOCK_DAMAGE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Mining..."}}]}

### PLAYER_EXPERIENCE_CHANGE
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: no
Fires when the player gets experience points (for example from an orb).
event values: EVENT_EXP_AMOUNT (NUMBER): the amount of experience points
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_EXPERIENCE_CHANGE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§a+%value(EVENT_EXP_AMOUNT)% XP"}}]}

### PLAYER_LEVEL_CHANGE
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player | cancellable: no
Fires when the player's experience level changes (up or down).
event values: EVENT_OLD_EXPERIENCE_LEVEL (NUMBER): level before; EVENT_NEW_EXPERIENCE_LEVEL (NUMBER): level after
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_LEVEL_CHANGE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§aLevel %value(EVENT_OLD_EXPERIENCE_LEVEL)% -> %value(EVENT_NEW_EXPERIENCE_LEVEL)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_VOTE
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who liked the world | cancellable: no
Fires when a player likes the world with `/like` while the world is in play mode.
notes:
- A player can like a world only once, so it fires once per player (a second `/like` is refused).
- If the player is in the code area, the selection is empty.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_VOTE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§d%player% liked the game! Thank you!"}}]}

### PLAYER_GOLD_TRANSACTION
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the paying player | cancellable: no
Fires after a successful creative-coin payment started by the action PLAYER_TRY_GOLD_TRANSACTION.
event values: EVENT_CREATIVE_COIN_TRANSACTION_AMOUNT (NUMBER): number of coins; EVENT_CREATIVE_COIN_TRANSACTION_NAME (TEXT): the transaction `name` (`none` if not given); EVENT_CREATIVE_COIN_TRANSACTION_DISPLAY_NAME (TEXT): the display name (the name if no display name)
notes:
- For the plot owner it fires at once and no coins are taken (test mode).
- For other players it fires only after the player confirmed and paid.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_GOLD_TRANSACTION","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§6Thanks for buying %value(EVENT_CREATIVE_COIN_TRANSACTION_DISPLAY_NAME)%!"}}]}

### PLAYER_AD
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who bought the ad | cancellable: no
Fires when a player in this world paid for a broadcast advertisement of this world.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_AD","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§b%player% advertised our game!"}}]}

### PLAYER_BUY_POINTS
event | category: PLAYER_WORLD | default target: the player who bought points | cancellable: no
Fires when a player in this world bought rating points for this world.
event values: EVENT_POINTS_PURCHASE_AMOUNT (NUMBER): the number of bought points (-1 outside this event)
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BUY_POINTS","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§eThanks for %value(EVENT_POINTS_PURCHASE_AMOUNT)% points!"}}]}

### PLAYER_SNEAK
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player starts to sneak.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_SNEAK","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Sneaking"}}]}

### PLAYER_UNSNEAK
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player stops sneaking.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_UNSNEAK","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Standing"}}]}

### PLAYER_SPRINT
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player starts to sprint.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_SPRINT","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§bSprinting"}}]}

### PLAYER_STOP_SPRINT
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player stops sprinting.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_STOP_SPRINT","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Walking"}}]}

### PLAYER_JUMP
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player jumps.
event values: EVENT_OLD_LOCATION (LOCATION): position before the jump; EVENT_NEW_LOCATION (LOCATION): position after the first jump step
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_JUMP","nodes":[{"action":"VARIABLE_INCREMENT","args":{"variable":{"var":"jumps_%player_uuid%"}}}]}

### PLAYER_MOVE
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player moves into another block: the block X, Y or Z of the position changed (walking, falling and jumping all count). Turning the head without moving does not fire it.
event values: EVENT_OLD_LOCATION (LOCATION): exact position before the step; EVENT_NEW_LOCATION (LOCATION): exact position after the step
notes:
- Teleports do not fire it.
- Cancelling moves the player back to the old position.
- Fires very often. Keep the line short and avoid waits here.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_MOVE","nodes":[{"action":"IF_PLAYER_STANDING_ON","args":{"blocks":{"item":"minecraft:emerald_block"}},"children":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§aCheckpoint!"}}]}]}

### PLAYER_TURNS_AROUND
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires on every movement packet in which the player's yaw or pitch changed (turning the head, also while walking).
notes:
- Fires extremely often (up to 20 times per second per player). Use it only for small checks.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_TURNS_AROUND","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Facing %value(CURRENT_PLAYER_FACING_VALUE)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_HORSE_JUMP
event | category: PLAYER_MOVEMENT | default target: the player riding the horse | cancellable: no
Fires when a horse (or similar mount) jumps and its first passenger is a player.
notes:
- Event entity (`DEFAULT_ENTITY`, `%entity%`) = the horse.
- Cancelling has no effect in this Minecraft version.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_HORSE_JUMP","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§6Hop!"}}]}

### PLAYER_INVENTORY_OPEN
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: no
Fires when the player opens an inventory window: a chest, a furnace, a menu made by code, a server menu.
notes:
- Opening the player's own inventory (E key) is not sent to the server and does not fire it.
- The type and title of the opened window: game values CURRENT_INVENTORY_TYPE and CURRENT_INVENTORY_TITLE.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_INVENTORY_OPEN","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Opened: %value(CURRENT_INVENTORY_TYPE)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_INVENTORY_CLOSE
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: no
Fires when the player closes an inventory window.
event values: EVENT_CLOSE_INVENTORY_REASON (TEXT): `PLAYER`, `OPEN_NEW`, `TELEPORT`, `DEATH`, `DISCONNECT`, `PLUGIN`, `CANT_USE`, `UNLOADED` or `UNKNOWN`
notes:
- Does not fire while the player switches mode (play / code / build).
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_INVENTORY_CLOSE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Closed (%value(EVENT_CLOSE_INVENTORY_REASON)%)"}}]}

### PLAYER_CLICK_INVENTORY
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player clicks a slot in an open inventory window, also number-key swaps, drops with Q and clicks on the crafting result.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item in the clicked slot; EVENT_CLICKED_ITEM (ITEM): the same item; EVENT_CLICKED_SLOT (NUMBER): slot index inside the clicked inventory; EVENT_INVENTORY_CLICK_TYPE (TEXT): `LEFT`, `SHIFT_LEFT`, `RIGHT`, `SHIFT_RIGHT`, `MIDDLE`, `NUMBER_KEY`, `DOUBLE_CLICK`, `DROP`, `CONTROL_DROP`, `SWAP_OFFHAND`, `WINDOW_BORDER_LEFT`, `WINDOW_BORDER_RIGHT`, `CREATIVE`, `UNKNOWN`; EVENT_CURSOR_ITEM (ITEM): the item on the cursor; EVENT_INVENTORY_ACTION (TEXT): what the click does, for example `PICKUP_ALL`, `PLACE_ALL`, `MOVE_TO_OTHER_INVENTORY`, `HOTBAR_SWAP`, `NOTHING`; EVENT_SLOT_TYPE (TEXT): `CONTAINER`, `QUICKBAR`, `ARMOR`, `RESULT`, `CRAFTING`, `FUEL`, `OUTSIDE`; EVENT_TYPE_OF_CLICKED_INVENTORY (TEXT): type of the clicked inventory, for example `CHEST`, `PLAYER`, `CRAFTING` (empty text when clicking outside the window)
notes:
- Cancel it to make a menu made with code read-only.
- When a server menu (not a code menu) is open, only clicks in the player's own inventory part fire it, and cancelling there has no effect.
- A click on the crafting result fires this event and also PLAYER_CRAFT_ITEM.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_CLICK_INVENTORY","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"},{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§7You clicked slot %value(EVENT_CLICKED_SLOT)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_DRAG_INVENTORY
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player drags an item stack over several slots.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the dragged item (cursor before the drag); EVENT_INVENTORY_DRAG_TYPE (TEXT): `EVEN` (left button, split evenly) or `SINGLE` (right button, one per slot)
notes:
- With a server menu open, cancelling has no effect.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DRAG_INVENTORY","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_PICKUP_ITEM
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player picks up an item from the ground.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the picked up item stack
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_PICKUP_ITEM","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§aPicked up an item"}}]}

### PLAYER_DROP_ITEM
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player drops an item (Q key or dragging it out of the window).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the dropped item stack
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DROP_ITEM","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"},{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§cYou cannot drop items here."}}]}

### PLAYER_SWAP_HANDS
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player swaps the items of the main hand and the off hand (F key).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item that goes to the off hand (it was in the main hand)
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_SWAP_HANDS","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_CHANGE_SLOT
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player selects another hotbar slot.
event values: EVENT_NEW_SLOT (NUMBER): new slot 0..8 (0 = left); EVENT_OLD_SLOT (NUMBER): old slot 0..8
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_CHANGE_SLOT","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Slot %value(EVENT_OLD_SLOT)% -> %value(EVENT_NEW_SLOT)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_CRAFT_ITEM
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player takes the result out of a crafting grid (crafting table or the 2x2 grid of the own inventory).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the crafting result
notes:
- The same click also fires PLAYER_CLICK_INVENTORY.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_CRAFT_ITEM","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"},{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§cCrafting is disabled."}}]}

### PLAYER_FURNACE_EXTRACT
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the world default (NOT the player who took the items) | cancellable: no
Fires when a player takes smelted items out of a furnace.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the furnace block as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the furnace; EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item type taken (count 1)
notes:
- The player who took the items is not available. The selection is one player of the world, which may be a different player.
- The same action also fires PLOT_BLOCK_EXP.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_FURNACE_EXTRACT","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§7Someone took items from a furnace."}}]}

### PLAYER_DAMAGE_ITEM
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when an item of the player loses durability.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item; EVENT_ITEM_DAMAGE (NUMBER): durability points it loses
notes:
- Event entity = the player. Cancel it to make tools unbreakable.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DAMAGE_ITEM","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_BREAK_ITEM
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: no
Fires when an item of the player breaks because its durability ran out.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the broken item
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BREAK_ITEM","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§cYour tool broke!"}}]}

### PLAYER_ITEM_MEND
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the Mending enchantment repairs an item of the player with an experience orb.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the repaired item; EVENT_EXP_AMOUNT (NUMBER): experience of the orb
notes:
- Event entity = the player. Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the experience orb.

### PLAYER_EDIT_BOOK
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player saves or signs a book and quill.
notes:
- No event values. Event entity = the player.

### PLAYER_MANIPULATE_ARMOR_STAND
event | category: PLAYER_INVENTORY | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player takes, puts or swaps an item on an armor stand.
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item in the player's hand; EVENT_ARMOR_STAND_ITEM (ITEM): the item on the armor stand; EVENT_ARMOR_STAND_EQUIPMENT_SLOT (TEXT): the armor stand slot: `HAND`, `OFF_HAND`, `FEET`, `LEGS`, `CHEST`, `HEAD`
notes:
- Event entity = the player. Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the armor stand.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_MANIPULATE_ARMOR_STAND","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_TAKE_DAMAGE
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the damaged player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player takes damage from any source (mob, player, fall, fire, lava, void and so on).
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage before armor and effects; EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage after armor and effects (1 = half a heart); EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): damage cause, for example `ENTITY_ATTACK`, `PROJECTILE`, `FALL`, `FIRE`, `FIRE_TICK`, `LAVA`, `DROWNING`, `VOID`, `ENTITY_EXPLOSION`, `BLOCK_EXPLOSION`, `MAGIC`, `POISON`, `WITHER`, `STARVATION`, `SUFFOCATION`, `CONTACT`, `CUSTOM`
notes:
- Damager (`"target":"DAMAGER"`, `%damager%`) = the entity that hit, when the damage came from an entity (for arrows it is the arrow). Shooter and projectile are set for projectiles. Victim (both kinds) = the player.
- Fires also when another server rule already cancelled the damage.
- For fall damage, PLAYER_TAKE_FALL_DAMAGE fires right after this event.
- To change the damage use `GAME_SET_DAMAGE_OF_EVENT`.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_TAKE_DAMAGE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§c-%value(EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE)% (%value(EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON)%)"}}]}

### PLAYER_TAKE_FALL_DAMAGE
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the damaged player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player takes fall damage (cause `FALL`). It comes right after PLAYER_TAKE_DAMAGE for the same hit.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage before armor and effects; EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage after armor and effects; EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): always `FALL`
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_TAKE_FALL_DAMAGE","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_DEATH
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the player who died | cancellable: no
Fires 5 ticks after a player died in play mode. At that moment the player is already alive again at the world spawn, and the items were dropped at the death place (unless the code saves the inventory).
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): the last damage cause of the player
notes:
- `CURRENT_LOCATION` here is the spawn, not the place of death. Store the position in PLAYER_TAKE_DAMAGE if you need it.
- No killer is set here. Use PLAYER_KILL_PLAYER or ENTITY_KILL_PLAYER, they fire right after this event.
- Does not fire for a spectator or a player with max health below 0.5.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DEATH","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§c%player% died (%value(EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON)%)"}}]}

### PLAYER_DAMAGE_PLAYER
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the attacking player | cancellable: yes
Fires when a player hits another player directly (melee). Projectiles do not count.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage before armor; EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage after armor; EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): damage cause (`ENTITY_ATTACK`, `ENTITY_SWEEP_ATTACK` and so on)
notes:
- Damager = the attacker. Victim (both kinds: `"target":"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the hit player.
- Fires only if the attacker is in play mode in this world.
- Fires also when another server rule already cancelled the hit.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DAMAGE_PLAYER","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§eYou hit %victim% for %value(EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_PROJECTILE_DAMAGE
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the player who was hit | cancellable: yes
Fires when a player is damaged by a projectile (arrow, trident, snowball, thrown potion and so on), from a player or a mob.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage (here the same as the final damage); EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE (NUMBER): final damage; EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): damage cause
notes:
- Event entity = the projectile. Damager and projectile = the projectile. Shooter (`"target":"SHOOTER"`, `%shooter%`) = who shot it. Victim (both kinds) = the hit player.
- For the same hit ENTITY_DAMAGE_PLAYER also fires.
- `GAME_SET_DAMAGE_OF_EVENT` does not change the damage in this event; use ENTITY_DAMAGE_ANOTHER_ENTITY for that.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_PROJECTILE_DAMAGE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§cYou were shot by %shooter%"}}]}

### PLAYER_KILL_PLAYER
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the killer (a player) | cancellable: no
Fires when a player died and the last entity that damaged them in the last 10 seconds was a player (directly or with that player's projectile). It runs 5 ticks after the death, right after PLAYER_DEATH.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): the last damage cause of the killed player
notes:
- Killer (`"target":"KILLER"`, `%killer%`) = the killer. Victim (both kinds) = the killed player.
- The kill counts also if the player died later from a fall or the void within 10 seconds after the hit.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_KILL_PLAYER","nodes":[{"action":"VARIABLE_INCREMENT","args":{"variable":{"var":"kills_%player_uuid%","save":true}}},{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§c%victim% §7was killed by §a%player%"}}]}

### PLAYER_DAMAGE_MOB
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the attacking player | cancellable: yes
Fires when a player directly damages a non-player entity (mob, armor stand, vehicle, painting, item frame).
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage (here the same as the final damage); EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE (NUMBER): final damage; EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): damage cause
notes:
- Event entity = the damaged entity. Damager = the player. Victim (both kinds) = the damaged entity.
- It can also fire with damage 0: setting the entity on fire (cause `FIRE_TICK`), a harmful splash potion (cause `MAGIC`), breaking a painting or item frame.
- `GAME_SET_DAMAGE_OF_EVENT` does not change the damage in this event; use ENTITY_DAMAGE_ANOTHER_ENTITY for that.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DAMAGE_MOB","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§eHit %victim%: %value(EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_DAMAGE_MOB_BY_PROJECTILE
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the shooting player | cancellable: yes
Fires when a projectile shot by a player damages a non-player entity.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE (NUMBER): damage (here the same as the final damage); EVENT_FINAL_DAMAGE (NUMBER): final damage; EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): damage cause
notes:
- Event entity = the damaged entity. Damager and shooter = the player. Projectile = the projectile. Victim (both kinds) = the damaged entity.
- `GAME_SET_DAMAGE_OF_EVENT` does not change the damage in this event; use ENTITY_DAMAGE_ANOTHER_ENTITY for that.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_DAMAGE_MOB_BY_PROJECTILE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§aNice shot!"}}]}

### PLAYER_KILL_MOB
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the killer (a player) | cancellable: no
Fires when an entity dies and its last damage was a direct hit by a player in play mode.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): the last damage cause of the dead entity
notes:
- Event entity = the dead entity. Killer = the player. Victim (both kinds) = the dead entity.
- It also fires when the dead entity is a player (then PLAYER_KILL_PLAYER fires too, 5 ticks later). Check the type if you need only mobs.
- If the last damage was not a hit (for example the mob burned to death after the hit), it does not fire.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_KILL_MOB","nodes":[{"action":"VARIABLE_INCREMENT","args":{"variable":{"var":"mobkills_%player_uuid%","save":true}}},{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§aKilled %victim%"}}]}

### PLAYER_KILL_MOB_BY_PROJECTILE
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the shooting player | cancellable: no
Fires when an entity dies and its last damage was a projectile shot by a player in play mode.
event values: EVENT_DAMAGE_REASON (TEXT): the last damage cause of the dead entity
notes:
- Event entity = the dead entity. Killer = the shooting player. Victim (both kinds) = the dead entity.
- It also fires when the dead entity is a player.
- For the same death ENTITY_DEATH_BY_PROJECTILE also fires.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_KILL_MOB_BY_PROJECTILE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§aShot down %victim%"}}]}

### PLAYER_CHANGE_VELOCITY
event | category: PLAYER_DAMAGE | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the server changes the player's velocity: knockback from a hit, a projectile or an explosion, or a velocity set by code.
event values: EVENT_VELOCITY (VECTOR): the new velocity
notes:
- Cancel it to remove knockback.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_CHANGE_VELOCITY","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_PICKUP_EXPERIENCE
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player picks up an experience orb.
event values: EVENT_EXP_AMOUNT (NUMBER): experience of the orb
notes:
- Event entity = the player. Plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the orb.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_PICKUP_EXPERIENCE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§a+%value(EVENT_EXP_AMOUNT)% XP"}}]}

### PLAYER_CHAT
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player who wrote | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player writes a chat message.
event values: EVENT_CHAT_MESSAGE (TEXT): the message text
notes:
- Commands (`/...`) and messages that start with `!` do not fire it. A message that is already cancelled when it reaches the code does not fire it.
- Cancel at the start of the line, before other work and before any `GAME_WAIT`.
- To compare the message use the condition IF_PLAYER_MESSAGE_EQUALS.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_CHAT","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"},{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§7[Game] §f%player%: %value(EVENT_CHAT_MESSAGE)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_LAUNCH_PROJECTILE
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player who launched | cancellable: yes
Fires when a player launches a projectile: arrow, trident, snowball, egg, ender pearl, thrown potion, fishing hook and so on.
notes:
- Event entity and projectile (`sel:"PROJECTILE"`, `%projectile%`) = the projectile. No event values.
- ENTITY_PROJECTILE_LAUNCH and ENTITY_SPAWN also fire for the same projectile.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_LAUNCH_PROJECTILE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§7Whoosh!"}}]}

### PLAYER_CONSUME_ITEM
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player finishes eating or drinking an item (food, potion, milk).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the consumed item
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_CONSUME_ITEM","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§aYum!"}}]}

### PLAYER_FOOD_LEVEL_CHANGE
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player | cancellable: yes
Fires when the player's food level is about to change (hunger goes down, or food raises it).
event values: EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the item the player is using right now (the food being eaten, else air)
notes:
- `CURRENT_FOOD_LEVEL` here is still the old level.
- Cancel it to keep hunger constant.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_FOOD_LEVEL_CHANGE","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLAYER_RESOURCE_PACK
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player | cancellable: no
Fires when the player's resource pack status changes after the world sent a resource pack.
event values: EVENT_RESOURCE_PACK_STATUS (TEXT): `ACCEPTED`, `DOWNLOADED`, `SUCCESSFULLY_LOADED`, `DECLINED`, `FAILED_DOWNLOAD`, `INVALID_URL`, `FAILED_RELOAD`, `DISCARDED`
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_RESOURCE_PACK","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§7Resource pack: %value(EVENT_RESOURCE_PACK_STATUS)%"}}]}

### PLAYER_BREED_ENTITIES
event | category: PLAYER_ANOTHER | default target: the player who fed the animals | cancellable: yes
Fires when two animals breed because a player fed them.
event values: EVENT_FATHER_UUID (TEXT): UUID of one parent; EVENT_MOTHER_UUID (TEXT): UUID of the other parent; EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the food used; EVENT_EXP_AMOUNT (NUMBER): experience that will drop
notes:
- Event entity and plain victim (`sel:"VICTIM"`, `%victim%`) = the baby animal.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLAYER_BREED_ENTITIES","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","args":{"texts":"§aA baby %entity% was born!"}}]}

### PLOT_WORLD_STARTS
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: no
Fires once when the owner (or a whitelisted builder) switches the world from build mode to play mode with `/play`. It runs after the code restart and after PLAYER_JOIN of every player.
notes:
- It does NOT fire when the code restarts for other reasons (import, first player entering an empty world). Do setup in PLAYER_JOIN (see the guide, section 5.7).
- The selection is one player of the world; it can be empty (for example the owner is coding). Use `SELECT_ALL_PLAYERS` or `"target":"ALL"` for messages.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_WORLD_STARTS","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§aThe game has started!"}}]}

### PLOT_WORLD_STOPS
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: no
Fires once when the owner (or a whitelisted builder) switches the world from play mode to build mode with `/build`. It runs before the switch, so the code still works; after it PLAYER_QUIT runs for every player.
notes:
- `GAME_WAIT` here never continues: the waits are cancelled by the switch.
- It does not fire when the server itself stops the code (for example after too many errors).
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_WORLD_STOPS","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§cThe game is stopping for building."}}]}

### PLOT_DATA_TRANSFER
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: no
Fires when this world receives data sent with `PLOT_DATA_TRANSFER_WRITE` or `PLOT_DATA_TRANSFER_WRITE_LIST` from another world of the SAME owner, with `target_plot_id` = this world's id. Only handlers whose `channel` equals the message channel run.
notes:
- The handler needs `"channel":"<name>"`. Without it the line never runs.
- Read the data with `PLOT_DATA_TRANSFER_READ`: `channel_name` may be empty, then the channel of this event is used; `output` is the variable that gets the data.
- Do not send data often; messages over the limit are lost.
- The selection is one player of the world (or the last spawned entity, or empty).
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_DATA_TRANSFER","channel":"scores","nodes":[{"action":"PLOT_DATA_TRANSFER_READ","args":{"channel_name":"scores","output":{"var":"received_scores"}}},{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§7Received: %var(received_scores)%"}}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_IGNITE
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the entity that started the fire (empty if a block started it) | cancellable: yes
Fires when a block is set on fire: flint and steel, fire charge, lava, lightning, fire spreading, explosion.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the block that caused the fire (for example lava or fire), NOT the block that starts to burn; nothing when an entity started it; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of that causing block; EVENT_IGNITE_REASON (TEXT): `FLINT_AND_STEEL`, `LAVA`, `SPREAD`, `LIGHTNING`, `FIREBALL`, `ENDER_CRYSTAL`, `EXPLOSION`, `ARROW`
notes:
- When a player uses flint and steel, the selection is that player.
- Cancel it to stop fire in the world.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_IGNITE","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_BURN
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when fire destroys a block.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the burning block as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the block
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_BURN","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_FADE
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a block fades away by itself: ice or snow melts, fire burns out, coral dies, farmland dries.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the fading block as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the block
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_FADE","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_PISTON_EXTEND
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a piston extends.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the piston as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the piston; EVENT_BLOCK_FACE (TEXT): the push direction (`UP`, `DOWN`, `NORTH`, `SOUTH`, `EAST`, `WEST`)
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_PISTON_EXTEND","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§7A piston pushed %value(EVENT_BLOCK_FACE)%"}}]}

### PLOT_PISTON_RETRACT
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a piston retracts.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the piston as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the piston; EVENT_BLOCK_FACE (TEXT): the direction of the piston
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_PISTON_RETRACT","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_EXPLODE
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a block explodes by itself (a bed in the Nether or End, a respawn anchor). TNT and creepers are entity explosions, see ENTITY_EXPLODE.
notes:
- No event values.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_EXPLODE","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_LEAVES_DECAY
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when leaves decay because no log is near.
notes:
- No event values.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_LEAVES_DECAY","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_STRUCTURE_GROW
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a sapling grows into a tree or a mushroom grows into a big mushroom (by itself or with bone meal).
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the block at the sapling position; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the sapling position; EVENT_TREE_TYPE (TEXT): tree type, for example `TREE`, `BIG_TREE`, `BIRCH`, `REDWOOD`, `JUNGLE`, `ACACIA`, `DARK_OAK`, `CHERRY`, `RED_MUSHROOM`, `BROWN_MUSHROOM`
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_STRUCTURE_GROW","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_MESSAGE","target":"ALL","args":{"texts":"§aA %value(EVENT_TREE_TYPE)% has grown."}}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_GROW
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a plant grows a stage: crops, sugar cane, cactus, melon and pumpkin stems.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the block before it grows; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the block
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_GROW","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_FROM_TO
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when water or lava flows into a new block, or a dragon egg teleports.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the source block (the flowing liquid); EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the source block
notes:
- Fires very often while liquid flows.

### PLOT_BLOCK_BREW

### PLOT_BLOCK_FORM
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a block forms by itself: snow falls, water freezes, lava and water make cobblestone or obsidian, concrete powder hardens. Snow golem trails and Frost Walker ice also fire it (and ENTITY_BLOCK_FORM).
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the block before it changes; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the block
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_FORM","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_SPREAD

Fires when a block spreads to another block: fire, grass, mycelium, mushrooms, vines, sculk.

### PLOT_BLOCK_PHYSICS
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a falling block entity (sand, gravel, anvil, concrete powder) changes a block: when it starts to fall and when it lands.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the block before the change (the falling block at the start; usually air at the landing place); EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of that block
notes:
- Normal block updates (redstone, water) do not fire it.
- The same change also fires ENTITY_CHANGE_BLOCK and ENTITY_BLOCK_STARTED_FALLING or ENTITY_BLOCK_FINISHED_FALLING.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_PHYSICS","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_CANCEL_EVENT"}]}

### PLOT_BLOCK_DISPENSE
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the world default | cancellable: yes
Fires when a dispenser or dropper shoots or drops an item (also when a dispenser puts armor on an entity).
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the dispenser or dropper as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the block; EVENT_ITEM (ITEM): the dispensed item; EVENT_VELOCITY (VECTOR): the velocity the item gets
notes:
- `GAME_SET_ITEMSTACK_OF_EVENT` changes the dispensed item. It runs once per target, so it needs a non-empty selection.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_BLOCK_DISPENSE","nodes":[{"action":"GAME_SET_ITEMSTACK_OF_EVENT","args":{"item":{"item":"minecraft:snowball"}}}]}

### PLOT_CAULDRON_LEVEL_CHANGE
event | category: PLOT_EVENT | default target: the entity that changed the cauldron (empty if none, for example rain or dripstone) | cancellable: yes
Fires when the liquid level of a cauldron changes.
event values: EVENT_BLOCK (ITEM): the cauldron as an item; EVENT_BLOCK_LOCATION (LOCATION): center of the cauldron; EVENT_CAULDRON_WATER_LEVEL_CHANGE_REASON (TEXT): for example `BUCKET_FILL`, `BUCKET_EMPTY`, `BOTTLE_FILL`, `BOTTLE_EMPTY`, `BANNER_WASH`, `ARMOR_WASH`, `SHULKER_WASH`, `EXTINGUISH`, `EVAPORATE`, `NATURAL_FILL`, `UNKNOWN`; EVENT_CAULDRON_WATER_LEVEL_CHANGE_OLD_LEVEL (NUMBER): level before (0 = empty, 3 = full); EVENT_CAULDRON_WATER_LEVEL_CHANGE_NEW_LEVEL (NUMBER): level after
notes:
- A full lava or powder snow cauldron counts as level 3.
example: {"kind":"event","event":"PLOT_CAULDRON_LEVEL_CHANGE","nodes":[{"action":"PLAYER_ACTION_BAR","args":{"message":"§bCauldron: %value(EVENT_CAULDRON_WATER_LEVEL_CHANGE_NEW_LEVEL)%/3"}}]}

### PLOT_SIGN_CHANGE

Fires when a player finishes editing the text of a sign.

notes:

### PLOT_BLOCK_EXP

Fires when a block drops experience: an ore is mined, a furnace is emptied by a player, a sculk block breaks.
